--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I'll analyze the mission and design 14 FSMs for the AGGREGATION mission.

## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:** The score increases by 1 for every robot outside the target region (the BLACK-floored strip with BLUE walls). Robots need to find and stay in the BLACK-floored target strip. 

**Key perceptions:**
- Floor color: BLACK = target strip (or the other strip), GRAY = middle
- Wall colors: BLUE = target side, GREEN = wrong side
- The robot LED colors can help others identify states

**Strategy:**
- Robots should move toward the BLUE walls (color 3 = blue)
- When on BLACK floor, they should check if they see BLUE walls vs GREEN walls
- GoToColor with blue (clr=3) should attract to the blue-walled strip
- GoAwayColor with green (clr=2) should push away from green-walled strip

**Champion Analysis:** Score 18811 - uses GoAwayColor(cyan=6), Exploration, FlockCenter, Repulsion. The approach seems to not directly target blue walls.

**Better approach:** Use GoToColor targeting BLUE walls (color 3) as primary behavior, with floor-color transitions to stabilize once on BLACK floor.

**Plan:**
1. Copy champion exactly
2. Exploit champion with small parameter tweaks
3. Explore with blue-wall-targeting strategies

## Reasoning

**Champion structure:**
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3) — move toward blue walls
  - → State 1 if floor is BLACK (p=0.95) — arrived at a black strip
  - → State 1 if floor is GRAY (p=0.3) — occasionally stop in gray
  - → State 1 if sees color 3/blue (p=0.85) — sees blue, stop
- State 1: Stop — stay still
  - → State 0 if floor is GRAY (p=0.7) — drift back to gray, go again
  - → State 1 if sees color 2/green (p=0.6) — stay stopped if sees green? (loops to self... wait, destination 1 from state 1 loops?)

Wait - from state 1, transitions go to destinations indexed among OTHER states. With 3 states, from state 1 the others are [0, 2]. So destination 0 → state 0, destination 1 → state 2.

- State 1: Stop
  - → State 0 if GRAY (p=0.7)
  - → State 2 if sees green (p=0.6)
- State 2: GoAwayColor(green=2)
  - → State 0 if sees blue (p=0.9)
  - → State 1 if GRAY (p=0.5)

This makes sense! Robots go to blue walls, stop on black floor, flee green walls if needed.

**Exploration ideas:** Add FlockCenter to help aggregate, use more transitions, try different color combinations.

- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3) — move toward blue walls
  - → State 1 if BLACK floor (p=0.95) — arrived, stop

  - → State 1 if GRAY floor (p=0.25) — occasionally stop in gray
- State 1: Stop
  - → State 0 if GRAY (p=0.7) — resume seeking if drifted to gray
  - → State 2 if sees green (p=0.6) — flee green walls
  - → State 2 if BLACK (p=0.2) — sometimes flee from black? (could be wrong strip)
- State 2: GoAwayColor(green=2)
  - → State 0 if sees blue (p=0.9) — blue visible, go toward it
  - → State 1 if GRAY (p=0.5) — stop if in gray
- State 3: FlockCenter(40)
  - → State 0 if GRAY (p=0.6)
  - → State 1 if BLACK (p=0.8)

State 3 (FlockCenter) seems underutilized — nothing transitions TO it. This is a weakness. I should create versions where state 3 is reachable.

**Key insights:**
- The core loop (GoToBlue → Stop on Black → FleeGreen → GoToBlue) works well
- State 3 (FlockCenter) is orphaned — need to add transitions TO it
- The "see green → flee" mechanism effectively distinguishes the wrong strip from the right one

**Exploration:** Try different arrangements where FlockCenter is actively used, try Attraction behavior in place of FlockCenter.

State 3 (FlockCenter) is still orphaned! The core 3-state loop (GoToBlue → Stop → FleeGreen) is working well. State 3 adds no value if unreachable.

**Key observations:**
- The probabilities in state 1 transitions to state 2 (green detection p=0.55, black p=0.25) help escape the wrong black strip
- The GoAwayColor(green) effectively pushes robots from the green-walled strip
- FlockCenter is dead weight — but maybe it sometimes gets activated by chance? Actually no — transitions are FROM states, not TO. State 3 is genuinely unreachable.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion exact
2. Exploit: make state 3 reachable by adding transitions to it from other states; try FlockCenter as active part of loop
3. Try replacing orphaned state 3 with something useful
4. Try 3-state versions of the core loop (dropping orphaned state)
5. Try different fc values, probability tweaks

## Reasoning

The champion hasn't improved from round 4 to round 5 (still 3029.65). We may be near a local optimum with this topology. Let me think more carefully.

**Champion analysis:**
- State 3 (FlockCenter) is still orphaned — nothing transitions TO it. It's dead weight.
- The effective FSM is really 3 states: GoToBlue → Stop → FleeGreen → GoToBlue
- The core logic: find blue walls, stop on black floor, flee if you see green walls (wrong side)

**Why isn't it improving?** The score of ~3030 out of a max of 20*1200=24000 means robots are spending about 3030/24000 ≈ 12.6% of time outside the target. That's actually quite good. But we can do better.

**Problems with current approach:**
1. When stopped on black floor, robots might be on the WRONG black strip (green-walled side). The transition "stop → flee if sees green" handles this but p=0.55 might be too low.
2. The GoAwayColor(green) might overshoot past the target strip.

**New ideas:**
1. Make state 3 reachable — route from GoAwayColor to FlockCenter when seeing blue
2. Try higher probability on green-detection transitions from Stop state
3. Try GoToColor(blue) directly from FlockCenter
4. Try a design where robots in the target region attract each other
5. Try using LED colors so robots can signal their state to others
6. Try Attraction behavior instead of Stop for aggregating on the black strip

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion has been stuck at 3029.65 for 3 rounds now. We need to escape this local optimum. Let me think more carefully about what's actually happening.

**Deep analysis of the champion:**
- State 3 (FlockCenter) is COMPLETELY ORPHANED — no state transitions to it. It contributes nothing.
- The effective 3-state machine is: GoToBlue(3) → Stop → GoAwayGreen(2) → GoToBlue(3)
- Score 3029.65 / (20 × 1200) = 12.6% of time outside target

**Why might robots still be outside?**
1. Robots starting in the middle take time to reach the blue strip
2. Some robots may oscillate near the boundary
3. The GoAwayGreen behavior might push robots too far into the middle

**New directions to try:**
1. **Eliminate the orphaned state** — use a genuine 3-state machine
2. **Use LED colors** to signal "I'm in the target" so others can aggregate there (Attraction toward cyan-LED robots)
3. **Higher probability on green detection** from Stop state (currently 0.55 seems low)
4. **Try GoToColor(blue) with floor-check only** — simpler 2-state
5. **FlockCenter as the "stay" behavior** instead of Stop — keeps robots aggregated in target
6. **Try different flee behavior** — maybe Exploration instead of GoAwayGreen lets robots find blue faster
7. **Add transition from GoAwayGreen → FlockCenter** when on black floor (reached target side)

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion is stuck at 3029.65 for 4 rounds now. This is a persistent local optimum. I need to think more radically about what's limiting performance.

**Root cause analysis:**
- The champion's effective FSM is GoToBlue → Stop → FleeGreen (3-state loop; state 3 is dead)
- Score 3029.65 means ~12.6% of robot-steps are outside the target
- Key failure modes: (1) robots starting far from blue strip take time, (2) robots near the gray/black boundary oscillate, (3) GoAwayGreen might overshoot back to gray

**Fundamental rethink:**
- The mission says BLACK floor = both strips, GRAY = middle. So floor color alone can't distinguish the correct strip from the wrong one.
- The only reliable discriminator is wall color: BLUE walls = correct side, GREEN walls = wrong side.
- Currently: GoToBlue works, FleeGreen works. But what about robots that are on black floor and can't see any wall? They stay stopped.
- **Key insight**: When stopped on black (correct strip), robots should signal their presence so others can join them. LED colors could help if other robots use camera to detect LEDs.
- **Another insight**: Maybe Attraction toward robots already in the target could help latecomers.

**New strategies:**
1. Use LED signaling: stopped robots emit cyan (6), moving robots emit magenta (5), so others can move toward settled robots
2. Replace Stop with FlockCenter in the "settled" state — keeps cluster tight
3. Try higher green-detection probability from stop state
4. Try a design where GoAwayGreen transitions to FlockCenter when on black (arrived at correct side)
5. Try completely different topology: Exploration → GoToBlue when sees blue → Stop when black

## Reasoning

Finally a small improvement (3029.65 → 3002.48)! The new champion added a 4th transition from Stop state: "→ State 2 if sees blue (p=0.85)" — this means when stopped and seeing blue walls, also go to GoAwayGreen. Wait, let me re-read.

**Champion analysis:**
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3)
  - → State 1 (Stop) if BLACK (p=0.95)
  - → State 2 (GoAwayGreen) if sees green (p=0.9)
  - → State 1 (Stop) if GRAY (p=0.3)
- State 1: Stop
  - → State 0 (GoToBlue) if GRAY (p=0.65)
  - → State 2 (GoAwayGreen) if sees green (p=0.55)
  - → State 2 (GoAwayGreen) if BLACK (p=0.25)
  - → State 2 (GoAwayGreen) if sees BLUE (p=0.85) ← NEW transition
- State 2: GoAwayColor(green=2)
  - → State 0 (GoToBlue) if sees blue (p=0.9)
  - → State 1 (Stop) if GRAY (p=0.55)
- State 3: FlockCenter(35) — still orphaned

Wait — from State 1, transition n1x3: destination=2, condition=7 (sees color), l=3 (blue). So when stopped and seeing BLUE walls, go to GoAwayGreen? That seems counterintuitive... unless it's detecting the blue walls of the CORRECT strip and then the GoAwayGreen pushes away from green — but if you're already at blue, GoAwayGreen would move you further into the blue strip, which is correct!

Actually if you're on black and see blue walls, you're already in the target. GoAwayGreen from there would keep you away from green. This makes sense.

**Key improvement**: The extra "sees blue → GoAwayGreen" transition from Stop state gives robots a way to reorient if they've drifted to the boundary. State 3 is still dead.

**Exploitation strategy**: 
- Tweak probabilities of the 4-transition Stop state
- Try making state 3 reachable and useful
- Try higher probability on the new blue-detection transition from Stop

**Exploration**:

- Try adding more transitions to GoAwayGreen state

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
